### Assumptions verified and how

| Item | Verification |
|------|--------------|
| Bevy 0.19.1 APIs of the A1 follow-up table in DECISIONS (M6) | Read in the sources (paths in DECISIONS) and exercised by code that compiles and runs in the tests and smoke runs |
| Despawning the map entity removes the tilemap `bevy_ecs_tiled` spawned under it | `World::despawn` documentation (recursive over `Children`); smoke runs restart twice and end with the views and the map of the last match only; `bevy_ecs_tiled` logs `Map removed` |
| A restart reads no file again | the catalogue holds the checked grids; the asset reader serves only the bytes registered at startup (D16 item 3) |
| Bevy's runner reads `AppExit` only when its event loop waits, so one more update may follow the last frame | `bevy_winit-0.19.1/src/state.rs:735`; the smoke checks run exactly once, at the last frame |
| No dependency change | `git diff 20116fb -- Cargo.lock Cargo.toml crates/*/Cargo.toml` is empty |

### Deviations from PLAN.md

1. **One faction and one map are shipped.** The menu's faction and map rows list the skirmish file's entries; the shipped file has one faction and the demo map, because the second faction and three maps are M7's scope. The selection mechanism is tested with the two fixture factions and the pure menu functions with several entries per row.
2. **Two players per match** (`MATCH_PLAYERS`): the human player at a map's first start position and one computer player at the second.
3. **What the AI knows beyond its vision** is a recorded decision (D43 item 2): the static terrain of the map and the start positions of the match, as public match setup.
4. **Starting setup changed.** Both players start alike (a yard, 5 light tanks, 3 scouts); until M5 the human player had 18 units and the opponent 8 units and a gun turret. Earlier user verification items that name these are restated below.
5. **The win condition applies only to a match set up by `setup_match`**, so the M0 to M5 tests, whose players often own no building, are unchanged (D41 item 2).
6. **An additional end-to-end test with the shipped data** (`game/tests/m6_production_matches.rs`) beside the plan's fixture-data test (D43 item 10).
7. **New public API of `sim`:** `load_skirmish`, `load_ai`, `setup_match` and its types, `MatchState`, `MatchOutcome`, `AiPlayers`, `AiController`, `plan_starting_units` and `StartError` (moved from the game), `SimEvent::{PlayerDefeated, MatchEnded}`, `CommandQueue::iter`. No existing data schema changed (all existing files keep schema version 1).
8. **Files not listed in PLAN.md section 4:** `crates/sim/src/{ai,ai_defs,match_setup,skirmish_defs}.rs`, `crates/game/src/skirmish.rs`, the M6 test files and fixtures. `m6_definitions.rs` contains `unsafe` code (the counting allocator of D15).

### Tests changed (none removed, skipped, or weakened)

The starting setup moved from game constants to the skirmish file (D40), so the tests that read those constants now read the data. Each keeps its assertion or makes it stronger:

1. `units::tests::the_production_units_load_and_every_sprite_passes_its_check` and `buildings::tests::the_production_buildings_load_and_every_sprite_passes_its_check`: "the starting units (buildings) name defined types" now loops over every faction's groups (buildings) of the skirmish file.
2. `units::tests::the_production_data_loads_with_the_combat_file`: the last assertion said that the opponent starts with a gun turret. The opponent no longer starts with one (deviation 4); the assertion now requires the gun turret, still a building type with a weapon, to be in the computer player's build order, so the demo match still has a defensive structure that fires. All other assertions are unchanged.
3. `units::tests::the_demo_map_holds_every_starting_unit_on_its_own_free_cell`: now checks the match that the menu's first choices set up (every unit of every group on its own passable cell, none on a building, every group complete), and keeps its original assertions on the M2 groups, now given as a test constant (`DEMO_GROUPS`, the same values) to `sim::plan_starting_units`.
4. `units::tests::starting_units_that_do_not_fit_are_a_setup_error`: unchanged assertions; the groups are `DEMO_GROUPS`, and `plan_starting_units` and `StartError` come from `sim`.
5. `buildings::tests::the_demo_map_holds_the_starting_buildings_and_units`: now checks the match set up from the data (both factions' buildings placed, no unit on a building, both players with the file's starting credits) instead of `setup_economy` and the constants.
6. `sim_host::tests::demo_host` (helper) installs the match as the menu does; `match_setup_installs_the_loaded_match_or_ends_the_game_with_an_error` checks the same two outcomes through `install_match` and `begin_match` (the demo map holds the match; a 16×16 map ends the game with exit code 1 with nothing reported as spawned), plus that the previous simulation is kept.
7. `smoke::tests::the_demo_player_can_queue_a_building_with_its_starting_buildings`: the human player's buildings come from its faction. `the_demo_match_expects_all_of_its_starting_units` asserted `starting_unit_total() == 12 + 6 + 5 + 3`, the constant now removed; it now asserts that the expected totals are those of both factions' groups and buildings, and that the set-up match has exactly that many units and buildings.
8. `smoke::tests::PASSING` gained the M6 fields; `the_game_systems_share_no_state_without_an_order` now also builds `SkirmishPlugin`; `SimHostPlugin { seed: 0 }` became `SimHostPlugin` in three tests.

### Known debt (P2 proposals for the reviewer)

1. Termination of computer-player matches is measured, not proven (D43 item 9): two computer players that both keep armed units they never risk, or have nothing left to sell, could stall. The shipped and fixture data end in every measured pairing.
2. The computer player uses no randomness (the same play gives the same responses); the match seed changes only `state_hash` until something uses `SimRng`.
3. A ready building waits while no visible spot satisfies the placement rules (D43 item 4); the computer player does not move units to gain vision for it. With the fixture's small sights this delayed a 3 × 3 building by about 1,000 ticks before the ring rule was relaxed; it is not seen with the shipped data.
4. Waves use `AttackMove` only (no explicit `Attack`, no chase beyond weapon range, no retreat for repairs); harvesters are not targeted on purpose.
5. Balance: in the measured pairings easy sometimes beats hard, depending on the start position. The balance pass is M7's.
6. The menu shows ids (underscores as spaces), not display names; the end screen does not block clicks on the map, which after the end are refused by the closed sink (logged, error sound).
7. A match setup error (shipped data that does not fit a map) ends the game with exit code 1 instead of returning to the menu with a message; every shipped combination is tested (`every_shipped_match_can_be_set_up_for_every_faction_pairing`).
8. The terminal event log also writes the computer player's events and rejections (M4 remediation debt item 2).
9. Test time: `m6_production_matches` takes 52 s and `m6_ai` 40 s in a debug build; `cargo test --workspace` takes about 2.5 minutes.
10. Hotkeys work in the menu (for example `P` toggles the sink's pause flag); every match starts unpaused.

Debt status: resolved by M6: M0 item 3 (constant seed; D44 item 3); M3 review 1 P2-4 and D20 item 4, D27 item 3, D31 item 5 (starting setup in game code; D40); M1 review 3 P2-3 first item (the `misalignment` documentation); M3 implementation debt item 2 for computer players (their stopped harvesters are sent back to work; the human player's still wait for a command). Awaiting the user: M1 review 1 P2-5 (acknowledgement of match setup as setup, D40 item 5; item 12 below). Unchanged and still relevant: M5 review 1 P2-2 (minimap on maps with W + H > 128: no such map is shipped; it must be resolved before M7's maps), P2-4 (the `m4_combat` temporary file race: it did not make a validation command fail in this session's debug runs, so it was not touched), P2-1, P2-3, P2-5, and every other item of "Recorded debt to carry" of the M5 review decision. The linker warning (M0 item 1) and the `Skipped event Destroyed` warning (M0 item 2) were seen again.

### Requires user verification

Not review findings (PLAN.md section 12 item 2); they block only `m6-approved`. Run `cargo run --release -p game` with sound on. The 13 M6 items:

1. **Menu:** the game opens on a dark full-window menu with the title, four rows (Map `demo`, Your faction `vanguard`, Opponent `vanguard`, Difficulty `normal`), each with `<` and `>`, and Start and Quit; all text legible; the sidebar is not visible or clickable through it.
2. **Selection:** `<` and `>` cycle each row and wrap around (the difficulty through easy, normal, hard; the map and faction rows have one entry each, deviation 1).
3. **Start:** Start shows the demo map with the camera on your base: your construction yard at (15, 37) with 5 light tanks and 3 scouts beside it, credits 5000 in the sidebar; the music keeps playing.
4. **The computer player builds:** where you scout its base (the right half of the map, around (52, 42)), it has a power plant, refinery, barracks, war factory, and later gun turrets and a second refinery and power plant; its harvesters collect ore; its units gather a few cells toward the map's centre.
5. **It attacks and defends:** from about 3 minutes (normal) its army attacks your base in waves; units of yours that come near its base are attacked by its units at home and its turrets; a building of its that you destroy is built again.
6. **Difficulty:** on easy the first wave comes later (about 5 minutes) and smaller; on hard earlier (about 2 minutes) and larger.
7. **Defeat:** letting it destroy all your buildings ends the match: the simulation stops, DEFEAT is shown over the map with Restart and Main menu, and orders are refused with the error sound.
8. **Victory:** destroying all of its buildings shows VICTORY (its remaining units do not matter).
9. **Restart:** Restart starts a new match with the same choices: the old map, units, selection, control groups, and minimap content are gone, credits are 5000 again.
10. **Main menu:** Main menu returns to the menu; Start with other choices starts a match with them.
11. **Quit:** Quit closes the game.
12. **Acknowledgement (M1 review 1 P2-5):** match setup installs the map's grid and places the starting players, buildings, and units directly into the simulation before the first tick, not through `CommandQueue` (D11, D40 item 5); during a match the interface and the computer player change state only through `CommandQueue` (I5). Please confirm this reading of I5.
13. **Acceptance:** in complete matches started from the menu, you can lose to the computer player and you can defeat it.

The earlier items still pending (the 16 M5 items, the 9 M4 items, the 10 M3 items, the 7 M2 items, and the 5 M1 items) apply with the M6 starting setup: each player starts with its yard and 5 light tanks and 3 scouts (M2 item 1 named 18 and 8 units), the human player's title and sidebar show `power 0/20` at the start (unchanged), and the opponent has no gun turret at (50, 41) at the start (M4 items 3 and 7): its turrets appear as it builds them, and yours can be built from the sidebar. The opponent now acts (M5 item 15 said it would not until M6).

## M6 remediation round 1 report (2026-10-04)

This is the implementer session for remediation round 1 of M6 review 1 (`docs/reviews/M6-review-1.md`, NO-GO: 0 P0, 1 P1, 4 P2).

* **Branch and base:** `milestone/m6`, base `bdbaa5e`. Code commit `85b310f`; this report and the decisions are in the documentation commit that follows it.
* **Notes:** none accompanied the findings beyond the orchestrator's instruction to close each class of defect, with two examples: every cross-file reference in the new data files that is not validated at load, and every fallback the computer player takes when its data or state is unusable. Each finding was treated as a claim and reconciled against the code.
* **"Fails before" evidence:** before any fix, a temporary probe test (`crates/sim/tests/zz_probe_m6r1.rs`, removed again) ran five configurations against the reviewed code through `setup_match` and `load_ai`; all five were accepted (`Ok`): a start position outside the map with every building and unit on it, a start on rock, a computer player whose yard is walled in by rock, a computer player whose faction lacks the yard, and the reviewer's AI file without the factory. The game-level regression tests were run with the fix disabled (the P2-2 test with only the discard counter added, the loader test with the new check commented out) and failed; each passes with the fix.
* **Decisions:** DECISIONS D45, D46, and D47 (new section "M6 remediation round 1"), with amendments in place to D6 (M6 note), D40 items 2 and 4, D42 item 4, D43 items 5 and 6, and D44 items 1 and 4. The milestone table above is maintained by the orchestrator.

### Classes of defect

1. **Cross-file references of the new data files that were not validated at load** (P1-1, P2-1; D45 item 1 has the full audit table). Defective and fixed: start positions against their map's size and terrain (P1-1); a faction's buildings and units at every start of every map (implementation debt item 7, which the review attached to P1-1); the producers and prerequisites of the harvester and the army, the producers of the build order and of the power building, and the prerequisites of the power building (P2-1). Already checked: building and unit types, map files, build-order prerequisites.
2. **Fallbacks the computer player takes when its data or state is unusable** (P1-1; D45 item 3). Defective and fixed: the gathering point's fallback to the start position. Closed by the data checks of class 1: off-map enemy start waypoints; a build order, harvester, or army whose producer can never be owned. Unreachable by construction and left as they are (listed in D45 item 3). Remaining in play (not data): a computer player that loses a producer and cannot pay to rebuild it waits (termination debt, below).
3. **Paths on which commands leave the game without a trace or a failed check** (P2-2, P2-4; D47). Defective and fixed: the sink's silent `reset` (one logging discard path now); the smoke test's count of pushed rather than accepted computer-player commands.

### Findings

#### P1-1: start positions are never checked against their map, and the computer player falls back to a gathering point off the map

**Outcome: confirmed. Both classes are fixed.**

* **Evidence (code).** `skirmish_defs.rs` checked a start only against 0 to 255 and duplicates; `game::skirmish::load` called `check_map` for each map but never compared the starts with its grid; `setup_match` accepted any start at which the faction's offset buildings and groups fit; `AiController::new` took `destination_cells(grid, toward, 1, ..).first().unwrap_or(start)`, which is the off-map start when `toward` lies off the map, and the enemy's start became a priority waypoint. `smoke.rs` counted pushed commands.
* **Evidence (reproduction).** On the reviewed code, `setup_match` returned `Ok(MatchStart { buildings: 3, units: 7 })` for the fixture with faction 1's start at (52, 32) on the 40 x 40 map (its buildings and units at offsets that put them where the fixture has them), and the same for a start on rock at (36, 32); the computer player's gathering point was (52, 32). The game-level loader test with the reviewer's root (`(70, 42)` on the demo map) loaded the catalogue when the new check was disabled. After the fix, the real binaries (release and debug, `BEVY_ASSET_ROOT` scratch root as the reviewer's `p1_start_outside`) exit 1 at startup with `data file …/skirmish.ron: maps[0].starts[1]: value (70, 42), which is outside the 64 x 64 cells of map "demo" is not supported, expected a cell of the map with passable terrain`, peak footprint 0.14 GB.
* **Change** (D45; `crates/sim/src/{skirmish_defs,match_setup,ai,lib}.rs`, `crates/game/src/{skirmish,sim_host}.rs`):
  1. `sim::start_problem` and `sim::check_map_starts`: a start position is a cell of its map with passable terrain; the `DataError` names the file, `maps[i].starts[j]`, the cell, the map's size and id.
  2. `setup_match` checks every start position of the map before anything changes (`MatchSetupError::InvalidStart { start, cell, problem: StartProblem }`).
  3. The gathering point is the cell reachable from the start over passable terrain, free of buildings, nearest to the point toward the centre; without one, setup fails with `MatchSetupError::NoGatheringPoint` (no fallback). For the shipped and fixture data it is the same cell as before (a probe compared the two rules for every fixture and shipped pairing), so valid matches and their hashes are unchanged; the 20 against 20 battle still ends at tick 665 with hash `c9401425d786dac6`.
  4. The game's catalogue (`skirmish::check_catalogue`, called by `load`) checks every map's starts, the AI file against every faction (D46), and sets up every match the menu can choose once in a dropped simulation (`sim_host::check_match`), which also resolves implementation debt item 7 (a faction that does not fit now fails at startup: scratch root `no_fit`, start (62, 42), exit 1 naming the map, the factions, and the building). With eight factions the 64 trial setups take a few milliseconds in release (scratch root `eight_factions`, exit 0).
  5. The smoke test fails on any rejected computer-player command (P2-4).
* **Regression tests:**
  * `m6_match::a_start_position_that_is_not_a_passable_cell_of_the_map_is_a_setup_error`: (52, 32) off the 40 x 40 map and (36, 32) turned to rock, each with a human or computer player at either start; typed error, message, and state hash unchanged; the same buildings and units from a start on the map are accepted. Fails before the fix (setup returned `Ok`).
  * `m6_match::a_computer_player_without_a_reachable_gathering_point_is_a_setup_error`: the computer player's yard walled in by rock; `NoGatheringPoint { player: 1, start: (32, 32) }`. Fails before (the gathering point fell back to the covered start).
  * `m6_match::every_gathering_point_and_waypoint_of_a_computer_player_is_a_passable_cell_of_the_map`: guard for the derived cells (passes before and after).
  * `m6_definitions::start_positions_are_checked_against_their_map`: the reviewer's (255, 255) on the 40 x 40 fixture (still a valid value of the file itself, `limits_are_accepted_at_both_ends`), (40, 5), (−1, 0), and rock at (25, 9), each with file, field, found, and expected; the map's corners are accepted; a map index outside the file is an error.
  * `skirmish::tests::loading_rejects_a_start_position_outside_its_map`: the reviewer's scratch root built from a copy of `assets/` in a temporary directory, through `load`; then (58, 42) loads. Fails with the check disabled.
  * `skirmish::tests::the_catalogue_rejects_starts_off_their_map_unbuildable_ai_files_and_unplayable_matches`: off-map and rock starts, a faction without the yard, and a faction whose yard does not fit, each with the file and field or map and factions named; the production catalogue passes.

#### P2-1: the AI file's cross checks do not ensure that the army or the harvester can be produced

**Outcome: confirmed and fixed** (D46; D42 item 4 corrected).

* **Evidence.** `ai_defs.rs` checked only the build order's prerequisites; the army's and harvester's producers and prerequisites, and the producers of the buildings, were not checked. On the reviewed code the fixture AI file with the factory removed from the build order and `army: ["tank"]` loaded (`Ok(AiDefinitions { .. })`).
* **Change** (`crates/sim/src/{ai_defs,match_setup,lib}.rs`, `assets/data/ai.ron` comment):
  1. `load_ai`: the producer and every prerequisite of the harvester and of every army unit type must be in the build order; the power building must have no prerequisites (it is queued whenever power is short, from the first turn).
  2. `sim::check_ai_factions` (the game's catalogue) and `setup_match` (`MatchSetupError::UnobtainableProducer`, computer players only): the producer of every build-order building is a starting building of the faction or listed before it, and the power building's producer is a starting building.
  3. The shipped and fixture data pass unchanged. The reviewer's scratch root `p2_army_unbuildable` now exits 1 at startup in both profiles (`ai.ron: harvester: value "harvester", whose producer "war_factory" is not listed in the build order …`); a faction without the yard exits 1 naming `build_order[0]`, the producer, and the faction.
* **Regression tests:**
  * `m6_definitions::the_army_harvester_and_power_building_can_be_obtained_through_the_build_order`: the reviewer's case (no factory: rejected at `harvester`), no barracks (`army[2]`), a tank that needs a fence (an economy fixture variant, `army[0]`), and a power building with prerequisites (`power`). The first fails before the fix (it loaded).
  * `m6_definitions::the_ai_file_is_checked_against_every_faction_of_the_skirmish_file`: a faction without the yard (`build_order[0]`, naming the file, building, producer, and faction), a build order that builds the yard first, and the power building's own rule.
  * `m6_match::a_computer_player_whose_faction_lacks_a_producer_of_its_build_order_is_a_setup_error`: typed error, state unchanged; the same faction for a human player is accepted. Fails before (setup returned `Ok`).
* **Termination debt (not closed by this fix):** the checks keep the data from making a computer player unable to build; a player that loses a producer in play and cannot pay for it can still stall (recorded below).

#### P2-2: `uninstall_match` clears pending commands silently before `close()` can log them

**Outcome: confirmed and fixed** (D47 item 1).

* **Evidence.** `uninstall_match` called `reset` (`pending.clear()`, no log) and then `close` (which logs). `install_match` also called `reset`.
* **Change** (`crates/game/src/sim_host.rs`): one discard path, `CommandSink::discard(why)`, logs every dropped command with the reason and a running count (`discarded`); `reset` ("a new match starts") and `close` ("the match is over") use it; `uninstall_match` closes, then unpauses.
* **Regression test:** `sim_host::tests::commands_pending_when_a_match_is_replaced_are_discarded_with_a_log_line`: a command pending when a running demo match is ended is discarded through the logging path (count 1), and one pending when a match is installed as well (count 2). It failed on the reviewed order (`left: 0, right: 1`) with only the counter added to `close`, and passes with the fix.

#### P2-3: `a_run_with_another_seed_or_difficulty_diverges` tests neither a seed nor a different course

**Outcome: confirmed and fixed** (D47 item 3).

* **Evidence.** Both runs used seed 6 and differed only in player 0's difficulty; the test asserted only the first hash, which differs by construction (the difficulty is hashed).
* **Change** (`crates/sim/tests/m6_ai.rs`): renamed `a_run_with_another_difficulty_takes_another_course`; `run_match` records each computer player's commands; the test additionally asserts that player 0's command stream and the match length differ. All earlier assertions are kept. The seed claim is dropped (the computer players use no randomness, implementation debt item 2).
* **Test:** the strengthened test is the fix itself; a test of a test cannot fail before it. It passes (3.3 s).

#### P2-4: the smoke test's computer-player check passes when every command of the computer player is rejected

**Outcome: confirmed and fixed** (D47 item 2).

* **Evidence.** `smoke.rs` reported and checked `commands_issued()`, the pushed count, and failed only the human player's rejections.
* **Change** (`crates/game/src/smoke.rs`): `watch_events` counts the computer player's `CommandRejected` events in every match of the run, the final line reports `computer player's commands: N (M rejected)`, and any rejection fails the run (no loss can explain one in the short smoke matches).
* **Regression test:** `smoke::tests::a_run_passes_only_with_the_menu_the_game_flow_and_the_computer_player` gains the reviewer's case (10 pushed, 9 rejected) and a single rejection, both failing with `N commands of the computer player were rejected`. The reviewer's run is the evidence that the old check passed it; with the P1-1 fix the same data no longer reaches a match.

#### Implementer's claims named by the review

* **D42 item 4** ("keep a computer player from getting stuck"): corrected and narrowed to the data (D42 item 4 amended, D46).
* **D43 item 5** ("a computer player's command can be rejected only because of what happens in the tick between …"): now stated for data that passes D45 and D46, which the game checks at startup and `setup_match` checks for every match.
* **D44 item 4** ("discards pending ones with a log line, so nothing is lost silently"): now true on every path (D47).

### Tests changed (none removed, skipped, or weakened)

* New: `m6_match` 4 tests (+ helpers `computer`, `shifted_start`), `m6_definitions` 3 tests (+ helper `definitions_with_economy`), `sim_host` 1 test, `skirmish` 2 tests (+ helper `copy_dir`). Extended: `smoke::tests::a_run_passes_only_with_the_menu_the_game_flow_and_the_computer_player` (two cases, field `ai_rejected` in `PASSING`), `m6_ai::run_match` (records commands).
* Renamed and strengthened: `m6_ai::a_run_with_another_seed_or_difficulty_diverges` → `a_run_with_another_difficulty_takes_another_course` (P2-3; all assertions kept).
* **Two existing tests change their stimulus, not their assertions:**
  * `m6_definitions::limits_are_accepted_at_both_ends`: the longest build order was a power building and 31 turrets, which no longer provides the producers of the harvester and the army (D46); it is now the power building, refinery, barracks, factory, and 28 turrets, and still asserts that 32 entries load.
  * `sim_host::tests::match_setup_installs_the_loaded_match_or_ends_the_game_with_an_error`: the demo starts on a 16 x 16 map are now rejected first as start positions outside the map (asserted in addition); for the unchanged "starting building" assertion the plan uses starts (14, 1) and (1, 14) on the small map, where the yard still does not fit. The game-flow half of the test is unchanged.
* No fixture or shipped data value changed; `assets/data/ai.ron` gained comment lines stating the rules.

### Acceptance criteria and required tests (PLAN.md section 7, M6)

Unchanged from the M6 implementation report, with these updates:

### Assumptions verified and how

No new crate API is used. Four-neighbour flood fill connects the same cells as the pathfinder's steps: `pathfinding::neighbours` allows a diagonal step unless both orthogonal neighbours are blocked, so every diagonal step has an orthogonal detour through a passable cell. The gathering points of every fixture and shipped pairing equal those of the reviewed rule (probe test, removed). Bevy's asset root for the scratch runs is `BEVY_ASSET_ROOT`, as in the review.

### Deviations from PLAN.md

* No new deviation. Deviations 1 to 8 of the M6 implementation report stand. Deviation 7 (new public API of `sim`) grows by `check_map_starts`, `start_problem`, `StartProblem`, `check_ai_factions`, and three `MatchSetupError` variants; no data schema changed.

### Known debt (P2)

1. **Termination of computer-player matches** (implementation debt item 1, narrowed): the data cases of M6 review 1 P2-1 are closed (D46). A computer player that loses its army producer or its yard in play and has no credits to rebuild it can still stall, and `broke` does not sell to rebuild a producer; the shipped and fixture data end in every measured pairing.
2. **Start positions require passable terrain** (D45): a start whose own yard covers it must still be passable terrain, and a faction's yard must stand on buildable terrain, which the trial setup checks.
3. **The power building may have no prerequisites** (D46): a data restriction chosen over a rule that would order the build order around power.
4. **Startup cost of the catalogue check** grows with maps × factions² (at most 16 × 64 = 1,024 trial setups on maps up to 256 × 256); 64 setups on the demo map take a few milliseconds in release.
5. **Implementation debt item 7 resolved for catalogue data**: a setup error can no longer occur for a match the menu offers; `begin_match` keeps its exit-1 path as a second line.
6. **Carried, unchanged, from the M6 review decision**: implementation debt items 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, and 10; everything carried from M5, M4, M3, M2, M1, and M0. The linker warning (M0 item 1) was seen again; the `Skipped event Destroyed` warning did not appear in this round's smoke runs.

### Requires user verification

Not review findings (PLAN.md section 12 item 2); they block only `m6-approved`. The 13 M6 items listed under "M6 implementation report" remain pending unchanged (this round changes nothing on screen: the shipped data passes every new check, and the gathering points and courses of matches are unchanged). The earlier items of M5, M4, M3, M2, and M1 remain pending.

## M6 review decision (2026-10-04)

Two independent review rounds (`docs/reviews/M6-review-1.md`: NO-GO, 0 P0, 1 P1, 4 P2; `M6-review-2.md`: GO WITH DOCUMENTED NON-BLOCKING DEBT, 0 P0, 0 P1, 2 P2). Before review 2 the orchestrator reran all six validation commands of PLAN.md section 9 on commit `834efc0` with exit code 0 (394 tests passed). Under PLAN.md section 12 item 1, `milestone/m6` was merged into `main` and tagged `m6-reviewed`. The tag `m6-approved` is created by the user alone after the verification below.

### Debt recorded from review 2 (P2, verbatim from the report)

#### P2-1: The gathering point is searched over terrain only, through the computer player's own starting buildings, so it can be a cell none of its units can reach. The code comment and D45 item 3 claim otherwise.

* **File and line:**
  * `crates/sim/src/ai.rs:333` defines `terrain` as `grid.cell(cell).is_some_and(|t| t.passable)`, and line 351 uses it as the only traversal test of the flood fill in `gathering_point` (`ai.rs:323-359`). Cells covered by buildings are traversed; they are only excluded as candidates (`grid.building(cell).is_none()`).
  * `crates/sim/src/match_setup.rs:455` places the starting buildings before `AiController::new` is called (`match_setup.rs:513`). Those buildings are therefore on the grid when the gathering point is chosen. Units cannot enter them: `Grid::is_passable` is false under a building, and `pathfinding::neighbours` uses it as the enterable test.
  * `ai.rs:321-322` says "Its units can walk there unless buildings placed later block the way". `docs/DECISIONS.md:673` (D45 item 3) says the four-neighbour fill "connects the same cells as the eight-neighbour steps of equation 5".
  * `m6_match::every_gathering_point_and_waypoint_of_a_computer_player_is_a_passable_cell_of_the_map` (`m6_match.rs:583`) checks passability, not reachability from the computer player's units.
* **Evidence:**
  * **Map:** `simprobe2` on the demo map with the shipped data, and the terrain around the computer player's start changed (`rock:45,36,63,50; grass:45,42,51,44; grass:52,42,54,44; grass:55,38,62,48; grass:52,45,62,48`). The yard (52..54, 42..44) is the only link between a corridor that leads west to the rest of the map and an eastern pocket in which all 8 of its starting units stand.
  * **Checks pass:** setup succeeds. Every start is a passable cell and every check of D45 and D46 passes.
  * **The gathering point:** it is `(44, 39)`, west of the yard. No unit of the computer player can reach it.
  * **Human player who gives no orders against normal, 36,000 ticks:**
    * outcome `None`;
    * the computer player issues 1,990 commands, mostly one gathering order per turn;
    * 17,874 `MoveFailed { reason: Unreachable }` events;
    * 0 rejections.
  * **Normal against normal:** it ends at tick 13,979, with 5,984 unreachable moves.
  * **Debug and release:** the first 12,000 ticks give an identical digest (`89a4372ecd92da4d`).
  * **Real binaries:** the same map as a `.tmx` in scratch root `plugged` passes the catalogue and the smoke test (`--smoke-test 600`, exit 0 in both profiles) with `computer player's commands: 10 (0 rejected)`. The usual count is 2; the extra commands are the futile gathering orders, and no check notices them.
* **Why P2 and not P1:**
  * No command is rejected and no invariant is violated.
  * The data is a contrived map whose own starting buildings partition the start's region. In this case the computer player's army is walled in whatever its gathering point is.
  * No shipped or fixture map has this shape, and the termination debt is already recorded.
  * The defect is that the rule and its documentation promise reachability for the computer player's units, which the code does not deliver.
* **Expected behaviour:**
  * Flood-fill with the units' rule: passable terrain and no building. Start from the free cells around the starting buildings (or from the cells of the computer player's starting units). If no gathering point can be reached that way, return `NoGatheringPoint`.
  * Alternatively, correct the comment and D45 item 3 and record the limitation as debt.
  * In either case, add a test with a start whose starting buildings separate its units from the point toward the centre.

#### P2-2: D47's title says that nothing the computer player issues is lost unnoticed, but its commands queued in the last tick of a match, or pending when a running match is replaced, are dropped without a log line

* **File and line:**
  * `crates/sim/src/schedule.rs:61-68`: the `Ai` stage runs after `Commands` and before `WinCondition`. A computer player's commands therefore wait in `CommandQueue` until the next tick.
  * `crates/game/src/sim_host.rs:349-372`: `run_tick` stops ticking once an outcome is set.
  * `crates/game/src/sim_host.rs:214`: `uninstall_match` replaces `SimHost`, and so its `CommandQueue`.
  * `docs/DECISIONS.md:687`: the title of D47 is "Nothing a player or the computer player issues is lost or rejected unnoticed". Its item 1 covers only `CommandSink`.
* **Evidence:**
  * `simprobe2` printed the queue after the last tick. After 1 tick it held `[(0, "QueueProduction …"), (0, "AttackMove { units: [SimId(7), …")]`, and after 2 ticks the corresponding commands of player 1.
  * Commands pushed in a tick that ends the match, or in the last tick before the smoke flow or a restart replaces a running match, are never validated. They produce neither a `CommandRejected` event nor a log line.
  * The effect is benign, because the commands belong to a match that is over or abandoned. The class is the same as review 1 P2-2, on the computer player's side.
* **Expected behaviour:** narrow D47's title to the interface's sink, or log the commands left in the simulation's `CommandQueue` when a match ends or is uninstalled.

### Recorded debt to carry (verbatim from review 2)

**New in this review (P2):**

* **P2-1:** the gathering point's flood fill traverses the computer player's own starting buildings. On a map where they separate its units from the point toward the centre, the gathering point is unreachable for them: one futile gathering order per turn, and 17,874 unreachable moves in 36,000 ticks in the probe.
  * The comment at `ai.rs:321-322` and D45 item 3 claim that the units can reach it.
  * The fix is to flood-fill with the units' rule, or correct the documentation and add a test.
* **P2-2:** D47's title overclaims. Commands of the computer player that wait in `CommandQueue` when a match ends, or when a running match is replaced, are dropped without a log line. Narrow the title or log them.

**From the M6 remediation round 1 report (known debt), confirmed by this review:**

1. Termination is measured, not proven. A computer player that loses a producer (or its yard, which no build order lists) and cannot pay to rebuild it can stall. P2-1 above is a further data-shaped case.
2. Start positions require passable terrain (D45), and the yard must stand on buildable terrain (checked by the trial setup). The sand probe confirms that this case is a typed error.
3. The power building may have no prerequisites (D46).
4. The catalogue check's startup cost grows with maps × factions².
   * Measured worst case: 1,024 setups on 256 × 256 maps take 0.8 s in release and 29.5 s in debug.
   * With 2,048 starting units per setup, they take 1.8 s in release, and the reviewer estimates about 1 minute in debug.
   * The window shows nothing meanwhile.
5. Implementation debt item 7 is resolved for catalogue data; `begin_match` keeps its exit-1 path as a second line.
6. Carried from the M6 review 1 decision: implementation debt items 2 (no randomness), 3 (a ready building waits for a visible spot), 4 (`AttackMove` only), 5 (balance), 6 (ids in the menu; the end screen does not block map clicks), 8 (the terminal log writes the computer player's events), 9 (test time: `m6_production_matches` 52 s, `m6_ai` 40 s, workspace 153 s), and 10 (hotkeys work in the menu).

**Resolved by M6 and confirmed:**

* Review 1 P1-1 and P2-1 to P2-4.
* M0 debt item 3 (the constant seed).
* M3 review 1 P2-4, and D20 item 4, D27 item 3, and D31 item 5 (starting setup in game code).
* M1 review 3 P2-3, first item.
* M3 implementation debt item 2, for computer players only.
* M6 implementation debt item 7, for catalogue data.

**Carried unchanged from the M5 review decision:** every item of its "Recorded debt to carry", namely M5 review 1 P2-1 to P2-5 and M5 implementation debt items 1 to 8, the accepted deviations, the OFL note for M7 packaging, and everything carried from M4, M3, M2, M1, and M0. M6 did not make any of them worse:

* M5 P2-2 (the minimap for W + H > 128) still applies. The M6 catalogue accepts such maps (the reviewer's 256 × 256 roots load and play), but none is shipped. It must be resolved before M7 ships such a map.
* M5 P2-4 (the `m4_combat` temporary-file race) did not occur in this review's debug and release runs.

The linker warning (M0 item 1) appeared again.

### Requires user verification (blocks only `m6-approved`; verbatim from review 2)

Run `cargo run --release -p game` with sound on. These are the 13 M6 items of the implementation report; the remediation changes nothing on screen.

1. **Menu:** dark full-window menu, title, four rows (map `demo`, your faction `vanguard`, opponent `vanguard`, difficulty `normal`) with `<` and `>`, and Start and Quit. All text is legible, and the sidebar is neither visible nor clickable through the menu.
2. **Selection:** `<` and `>` cycle each row and wrap around (difficulty easy, normal, hard; one map and one faction).
3. **Start:** the demo map, with the camera on your base: yard at (15, 37), 5 light tanks and 3 scouts, credits 5000. The music keeps playing.
4. **The computer player builds:** around (52, 42) it builds a power plant, refinery, barracks, war factory, and later gun turrets, a second refinery, and a second power plant. Its harvesters collect ore, and its units gather toward the map's centre.
5. **It attacks and defends:**
   * waves from about 3 minutes on normal;
   * your units near its base are attacked by its units at home and by its turrets;
   * a building of its that you destroy is rebuilt.
6. **Difficulty:** on easy the first wave comes later (about 5 minutes) and is smaller; on hard it comes earlier (about 2 minutes) and is larger.
7. **Defeat:** losing all of your buildings stops the simulation and shows DEFEAT with Restart and Main menu; orders are refused with the error sound.
8. **Victory:** destroying all of its buildings shows VICTORY.
9. **Restart:** a new match with the same choices; nothing of the old match is left; credits are 5000 again.
10. **Main menu:** it returns to the menu, and Start with other choices uses them.
11. **Quit:** it closes the game.
12. **Acknowledgement (M1 review 1 P2-5):** match setup installs the grid and places the players, buildings, and units directly before the first tick, not through `CommandQueue` (D11, D40 item 5). During a match, the interface and the computer player change state only through `CommandQueue` (I5). Please confirm this reading of I5.
13. **Acceptance:** in complete matches started from the menu, you can lose to the computer player and you can defeat it.

The earlier pending items (16 for M5, 9 for M4, 10 for M3, 7 for M2, and 5 for M1) remain, with the M6 starting setup noted in the implementation report.

I4: `BTreeSet` rosters, filtered lists in file order; `the_game_systems_share_no_state_without_an_order` now includes the options and the `Last` schedule. I5: the options and the interface change the simulation only through `CommandSink`; rosters are match setup. I6/I7: checked on every tick of the soak. I8/I11: the skirmish file through the RON subset; the settings file through the game's own bounded parser (no third-party parser); typed errors throughout. I10: the plan's tests and one benchmark scenario use fixture data; the production tests check properties of the shipped game. I12: no new overflow-prone arithmetic in `sim`; dev builds keep overflow checks (D53) |

### Deviations from PLAN.md

1. **Skirmish file schema version 2** (D48), with the fixture and the shipped file updated in the same change, as section 10 requires. The AI file keeps version 1; its lists are filtered per faction (D49).

The benchmark and soak are examples of `sim` (run with `cargo run --release -p sim --example …`), because their budget applies to release builds and they must not run in `cargo test`'s debug profile.

4. **The dev profile optimises `sim`** (D53); debug assertions and overflow checks stay on.

Its assertions are unchanged.

* `skirmish::tests::menu_buttons_cycle_through_each_row_and_start_only_a_valid_choice`: an added assertion for the Options button.

## M7 review decision (2026-10-04)

One independent review round (`docs/reviews/M7-review-1.md`: GO WITH DOCUMENTED NON-BLOCKING DEBT, 0 P0, 0 P1, 6 P2). Before the review the orchestrator reran all six validation commands of PLAN.md section 9 and the three M7 commands (`cargo run --release -p sim --example m7_benchmark`, `cargo run --release -p sim --example m7_soak`, `sh scripts/package_macos.sh`) on commit `8cdd42e`, all with exit code 0 (414 tests passed). Under PLAN.md section 12 item 1, `milestone/m7` was merged into `main` and tagged `m7-reviewed`. The tag `m7-approved` is created by the user alone after the verification below.

### Debt recorded from review 1 (P2, verbatim from the report)

#### P2-1: The settings file's write path follows a symlink and blocks on a named pipe at `settings.txt.tmp`; the read path rejects both
* **File and line:**
  * `crates/game/src/options.rs:353-360` (`write_settings`): `std::fs::write(&temporary, …)` on the fixed name `settings.txt.tmp`, then `rename`.
  * The read side (`options.rs:312-331`) rejects non-regular files before opening, as D52 item 2 states. It also leaves a check-then-open window between `metadata` and `File::open`.
* **Evidence (debug and release):**
  * **Named pipe at `…/mygame001-smoke-<pid>/settings.txt.tmp`:**
    * The last log line is "no settings file … the defaults are used".
    * The process then blocks in `save_settings` (schedule `Last`) until the 40 s alarm kills it (exit 142).
    * There is no error and no smoke result.
    * In a normal run the same file at `~/Library/Application Support/MyGame001/settings.txt.tmp` would freeze the game the first time the player changes an option.
  * **Symlink at `settings.txt.tmp` pointing to a scratch `victim.txt`:**
    * The run passes (exit 0) and logs "settings written to …".
    * `victim.txt` now holds the settings text, so the file outside the settings directory was overwritten.
    * `rename` then replaces `settings.txt` with the symlink itself.
* **Why P2:**
  * Only a hostile file in the user's own Application Support directory triggers it. A leftover regular `.tmp` file from a crash is simply overwritten.
  * No privilege boundary is crossed.
  * It is the same class and severity as M1 review 3 P2-1 (a named pipe at the map path hangs at startup).
* **Problem with the documentation:** D52 item 3 ("No silent fallback or loss") and the module documentation describe only the read side.
* **Expected behaviour:**
  * Create the temporary file with `OpenOptions::new().write(true).create_new(true)` (O_EXCL, which does not follow a final symlink and never opens an existing pipe).
  * Use a unique name, or remove a stale entry first after checking with `symlink_metadata` that it is a regular file.
  * Report a failure as the existing typed write error.
  * Add a test with a named pipe and a symlink at the temporary path.

#### P2-2: The release package's bundle signature does not verify, the executable is arm64 only, and the README's advice for other Macs is unverified

* **File and line:**
  * `scripts/package_macos.sh:27` copies the linker-signed binary into a bundle and never re-signs it.
  * `README.md:58`: "on another Mac, open it with a right click and **Open** the first time".
  * `docs/DECISIONS.md` D54 item 3 says Gatekeeper "asks on first open on another Mac".
* **Evidence:**
  * `codesign -dv target/package/MyGame001.app` reports `flags=0x20002(adhoc,linker-signed)`, `Info.plist=not bound`, `Sealed Resources=none`.
  * `codesign --verify` and `spctl --assess` both report "code has no resources but signature indicates they must be present".
  * `file` reports `Mach-O 64-bit executable arm64`.
  * A scratch copy re-signed with `codesign --force --deep -s -` verifies ("valid on disk", "satisfies its Designated Requirement").
* **Why P2:**
  * The version 1.0 target is the user's Mac (U1, PLAN.md section 12 item 3). A locally built bundle carries no quarantine attribute and opens.
  * D54 item 3 and user-verification items 9 and 10 already leave signing and distribution to the user.
  * The defect is that the package as built has an invalid bundle signature and an unverified claim about other Macs.
* **Expected behaviour:**
  * Ad-hoc sign the assembled bundle in the script and have the script fail unless `codesign --verify` passes.
  * State that the package is arm64 only, or build a universal binary.
  * Verify the README instruction on a second Mac, or reword it.

#### P2-3: STATUS lists M6 implementation debt item 9 (test time) as resolved ("reduced"), but test time grew

* **File and line:** `docs/STATUS.md:2435` ("M6 implementation debt item 9, test time, reduced (D53)"); D53.
* **Evidence:**
  * M6 debt item 9 recorded `m6_production_matches` 52 s, `m6_ai` 40 s, and workspace 153 s (`STATUS.md:2273`).
  * On M7: `m6_production_matches` 119.3 s, `m6_ai` 39.1 s, and `cargo test --workspace` 212.6 s (this review) or 189 s (implementer).
  * The time per match fell, but the debt (the time the suite takes) is larger.
* **Expected behaviour:** carry item 9 as open with the M7 numbers, or say "time per match reduced; total test time 189–213 s".

#### P2-4: The benchmark does not show that 200 units moved, and its headline mean is mostly post-battle ticks

* **File and line:** `crates/sim/examples/m7_benchmark.rs:271-274` (`moved` counts only units that survive the window) and `:431` (the pass criterion is `moved > 0`).
* **Evidence:**
  * The fixture scenario reports "28 units moved" out of 200.
  * Units alive are `[200, 147, 34, 28, 28]`, so about 1,000 of the 2,000 measured ticks run with at most 34 units.
  * The reported mean (0.078 ms) is about a third of the busy-part mean (0.248 ms).
  * The plan asks for "200 moving and fighting units".
* **Why P2:**
  * The busy-part mean is also held to the budget, and it stays far inside it (0.25 and 0.36 ms against 5 ms, a margin of more than 13×).
  * The scenarios force movement: the anchors are about 23 cells apart and every weapon range is at most 16 cells.
  * The budget decision therefore stands; only the evidence is weaker than its description.
* **Expected behaviour:**
  * Count units that ever changed position, including destroyed ones, and require that most of the 200 did.
  * Either end the measured window when the battle ends, or label the headline mean as the window mean.

#### P2-5: The automated end-to-end runs of the real binary start matches only on the first map

* **File and line:** `crates/game/src/smoke.rs:188` onward (`play_flow`): every match of the flow uses `MenuSelection`'s map 0 (`demo`). `MOVE_TARGET` (`smoke.rs:69`) is a demo cell.
* **Evidence:**
  * `crossroads` and `riverlands` reach `bevy_ecs_tiled` and the renderer only in manual play. The catalogue checks their files at startup, and `m7_maps` and the simulation tests cover them without rendering.
  * The reviewer reordered the maps in scratch roots, and `--smoke-test 600` passed on each new map in both profiles. The code path works, but section 9's commands do not cover it.
* **Expected behaviour:** let the smoke flow's three matches use three different maps (for example, Restart on the next map and the last match on the third), with a move target valid on each.

#### P2-6: The roster-limit boundary is tested at 73 entries, not at the limit

* **File and line:** `crates/sim/tests/m7_factions.rs:254-265`. The case appends 64 entries to a 9-entry list.
* **Evidence:** the report lists a "64-entry limit" boundary test, but no case loads exactly 64 or rejects exactly 65. A 65-entry list of repeated names reaches `TooManyEntries` before uniqueness, so the exact case needs no extra definitions.
* **Expected behaviour:** add the exact 65-entry rejection, or correct the report's wording.

#### Observation, not a finding

* The AI file keeps schema version 1 although the meaning of its lists changed: entries are now filtered by roster, and dead entries are rejected.
* D49 item 3 records and justifies this. The fields are unchanged, and the shipped and fixture files were updated and checked.

### Recorded debt to carry (verbatim from review 1)

**New in this review (P2):**

* **P2-1:** the settings write path follows a symlink and blocks on a named pipe at `settings.txt.tmp` (`options.rs:353-360`). Fix: create the temporary file with `create_new` and a unique name, and add a test.
* **P2-2:**
  * The package's bundle signature does not verify (`codesign --verify`: "code has no resources but signature indicates they must be present").
  * The executable is arm64 only.
  * The README's right-click advice for other Macs is unverified.
  * Fix: ad-hoc sign and verify in the script, and document the architecture.
* **P2-3:** M6 implementation debt item 9 (test time) is not resolved. The workspace now takes 189–213 s (M6: 153 s), and `m6_production_matches` 119 s. Carry it as open.
* **P2-4:** the benchmark's `moved` counts only survivors, its pass criterion needs one moving unit, and its headline mean is diluted by post-battle ticks. The busy-part mean (0.25 / 0.36 ms) is the meaningful figure.
* **P2-5:** the smoke flow starts matches only on the first map. The two new maps reach the renderer only in manual play (the reviewer's reordered-root probes pass).
* **P2-6:** the roster limit is tested at 73 entries, not exactly 65. The report's "64-entry limit" boundary claim overstates the test.

**New in M7, confirmed by this review (from the implementation report's known debt):**

1. The package is not signed or notarised, and the crate licence notices are not shipped (D54 item 3). See also P2-2 and user verification items 2 and 3.
2. The package is 116 MB; the executable is not stripped.
3. The art is procedural; its acceptance as final art is the user's (D50 item 3).
4. Balance is measured only between computer players (D51 item 5). Reproduced exactly by the reviewer's probe. Termination is still measured, not proven.
5. Dock contention (M3 review 1 P2-2) remains (D57 item 4).
6. The end rule's speed dependence in jams (M2 review 3 P2-1) remains (D57 item 5).
7. The benchmark does not measure the rendering side (M5 implementation debt 6). The minimap image of the largest map is 1,024 × 512. A 256 × 256 smoke run passed in both profiles.
8. The menu shows ids, not display names (M6 debt item 6).
9. The crate version stays 0.1.0.
10. `scripts/package_macos.sh` needs a display.

**Resolved by M7 and confirmed:**

* M0 debt item 7 and D1's packaging note: the package smoke test passes without the cargo variables.
* The OFL note of the M5 review: the OFL text is byte-identical to the pinned source and ships in the package.
* M5 review 1 P2-2: minimap scale of at least 2, a test, and a 256 × 256 map in the real binary.

* The measurements deferred to "the M7 benchmark" (M2 round 2 item 3, M3 debt 4, M4 debt 1).

**Not resolved, despite the report:** M6 implementation debt item 9 (P2-3).

## M8 implementation report (2026-10-04)

Scope: PLAN.md section 13 and user decision U3. Implementation commit `3e302c2` replaces the
placeholder presentation with original industrial pixel art; game rules and production data are
unchanged. This is the visual-first follow-up, not completion of version 1.0 or user approval.

### Files changed

* Four existing Rust generators plus `examples/art/painter.rs` and `art/terrain.rs`: eight
  distinct industrial buildings, eleven shaded unit sheets, and the three shipped tilesets.
  All 22 generated PNGs retain their sizes and existing PNG/geometry/facing contracts.
* `presentation.rs`, `units.rs`, `buildings.rs`, `skirmish.rs`: checked sprite decoding and
  material-preserving team paint variants; menu sprite lineup, title hierarchy and DEPLOY label.
* `sidebar.rs`: compact two-column sprite cards, costs/badges/progress, military colors and borders.
* `visual_review.rs`, `main.rs`: opt-in native renderer capture mode with ordinary shipped starts
  and a separately labelled developed-base setup; five captures under `docs/screenshots/`.
* `scripts/package_macos.sh`: respect the configured Cargo target directory for executable and
  output paths. README, asset provenance and DECISIONS D58–D60 document these changes.

### Validation

The first mandatory Clippy attempt exited 101 on two style lints: an orphaned generator doc
comment and `chunks_exact_mut(4)` instead of `as_chunks_mut::<4>().0`. Both were reported, then
corrected within the authorized scope; the final Clippy rerun exited 0. No lint was suppressed.
During normal development, the scout facing-marker test caught an occluded back-facing marker;
the generator was corrected and all original facing assertions pass unchanged. Native menu
execution caught a duplicate TextColor bundle; an executable ECS spawn regression was added.
The first developed-base capture also revealed that extending a shared starting faction extended
the opponent; the isolated review setup now uses different factions and has a valid-match
regression. These development failures are resolved, not omitted from the record.

The full suite was rerun after adding that last regression. Logs are retained at
`/Users/xingyuwang/Library/Caches/Codex/reviews/MyGame001/m8/`.

| Command | Exit | Evidence |
|---------|------|----------|
| `cargo fmt --all -- --check` | 0 | Final tree clean. |
| `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` | 0 | Final rerun clean. |
| `cargo test --workspace` | 0 | 417 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored; 186.53 s. `workspace-tests.log`. |
| `cargo build --release -p game` | 0 | Optimized Apple Silicon build. |
| `cargo run --release -p game -- --smoke-test 600` | 0 | `release-smoke.log`; native GPU/display available. |
| `cargo run -p game -- --smoke-test 600` | 0 | `debug-smoke.log`; native GPU/display available. |
| `cargo run --release -p sim --example m7_benchmark` | 0 | Fixture mean/busy/max 0.081/0.262/0.857 ms; shipped 0.159/0.367/0.908 ms; peak heap 0.6 MiB. `benchmark.log`. |
| `cargo run --release -p sim --example m7_soak` | 0 | 3,168,000 ticks, 44 configurations twice, 151.5 s, peak heap 1.6 MiB; all checks passed. `soak.log`. |
